def optimize(p, seed, time_limit):
    n=len(p); p=p.copy(); p=np.clip(p,0,None); p=norm1(p)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score_fft(p)[0]
    beta_scale maybe adapt.
    step_sizes=[...]
    for iterations while time:
        q=convfft(p)
        qmax=q.max(); qmin? 
        # choose temperature: tau = max(0.05*qmax, 1e-12)
        beta = 1/tau
        w = exp((q-qmax)/tau); w[:]=1 sum? 
        grad = gradient_fft(p,w,n)
        # normalize gradient? grad scale ~ q? p scale. Use relative grad = grad / (p.mean()+eps)
        for eta in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0] maybe descending:
            logp = np.log(p+1e-30) - eta*grad*eta_scale
            newp = exp(logp-logp.max()); newp/=sum
            sc=score_fft(newp)[0]
            if sc < best_score: accept
        # if no accept, reduce eta or perturb
        # also try additive: newp = p - eta*grad; clip; norm
    return best

General family: p_i = (1 + α cos(2π (r i / n) + φ)) / n. Sum =1 if mean of cos over 0..n-1 zero. For integer r not multiple of n, sum exactly 0 (unless n divides r). Nonnegative requires α≤1 (or clip). α can be up to1. The linear convolution q_k can be computed in closed form! Product of cos:
p_i p_j = 1/n^2 [1 + α(cos_i+cos_j) + α^2 cos_i cos_j]
=1/n^2 [1 + α(cos_i+cos_j) + (α^2/2)(cos(2r(i+j? no) k/n + 2φ) + cos(2r(2i-k)/n + 2φ? let's derive)]
Let θ_i = 2π r i/n + φ.
θ_i + θ_{k-i} = 2π r k/n + 2φ.
θ_i - θ_{k-i} = 2π r (2i-k)/n.
cos_i cos_j = 0.5[cos(2π r k/n + 2φ) + cos(2π r (2i-k)/n)].
Thus q_k = m_k/n^2 + α/n^2 ∑_{valid i} [cos θ_i + cos θ_{k-i}] + α^2/(2n^2) [m_k cos(2π r k/n +2φ) + ∑_{valid i} cos(2π r (2i-k)/n)].
The last sum over i is a Dirichlet kernel: ∑_{i=a}^{b} cos((2i-k) t) with t=4π r/n? Actually 2πr(2i-k)/n. This sum can be expressed in O(1). First-order sums of cos over consecutive ranges also O(1) except if frequency 0. For r=1, these sums are O(1) generally; at special valid ranges maybe O(1) not O(n). So closed form exact possible. But easier to use FFT for evaluation/tuning. n=4096, scanning many parameters cheap.

Let's implement scoring for many candidates efficiently:
```
def next_pow2(m): return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()

def score_fft(p, cache_N=None):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    N=next_pow2(L)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    Q=np.fft.irfft(P*P,N)[:L]
    # clip tiny negative
    return 2*n*float(np.max(Q))
```
For scanning p with same n,N, we can reuse N. Building p via sin. Use `np.sin(np.arange(n)*omega + phase)`.

Let's write top-peaks gradient carefully:
```
def top_peaks_grad(p, q, K=32, temp=None):
    n=len(p); L=len(q)
    # choose top K indices
    if K >= L: idx=np.arange(L); weights = q/ q.sum()
    else:
       # topk
       idx = np.argpartition(q, -K)[-K:]
       idx.sort()
       sel = q[idx]
       if temp is None: temp = max(1e-15, 0.1*float(np.max(sel)))
       weights = np.exp((sel - sel.max())/temp)
       weights /= weights.sum()
    grad = np.zeros(n)
    for k, w in zip(idx, weights):
       w=float(w)
       start = max(0, k-(n-1))
       stop = min(n-1, k)
       # i from start..stop, j=k-i from k-stop..k-start
       i_slice = slice(start, stop+1)
       j_slice = slice(k-stop, k-start+1) # p[j_slice]
       # p_j reversed? Let's verify: i increasing, j=k-i decreasing. p[j_slice] with j_slice increasing from k-stop to k-start corresponds i stop down to start. We need grad[i=start] += w p[k-start]; grad[i=stop]+=w p[k-stop]. So use p[j_slice][::-1]
       grad[i_slice] += w * p[j_slice][::-1]
       # extra if 2i=k: i=k//2 if integer and within range
       if k % 2 == 0:
           ii = k//2
           if 0 <= ii < n:
               grad[ii] += w * p[ii]
    return grad
```
But weights sum 1, grad scale ~1/n? If p mean=1/n. For top K, weights=1/K maybe, grad ~ (1/K)*p ~1/(K n). So relative g=grad/p.mean ~1/K. Step eta should scale? If we use top peaks only, to get same effect as full softmax, eta should be multiplied by K? We can just choose eta larger. Or normalize grad by q? Maybe for top-peaks, use weights normalized but there are K peaks; derivative of sum of maximum? If using unweighted average of top gradients, step size may need larger. We can set `grad *= K`? But if K=32, eta times. Alternatively use weights as softmax over all q but approximate; sum weights 1, so same scale. If only top K, each weight ~1/K, so gradient smaller. Use `eta` grid [0.5..20] to account. Or normalize weights to sum 1 and scale by K? Let's do both: weights sum1; update with eta list [1,3,10,30,100]? Since raw grad ~1/(K n), relative ~1/K. eta=K gives ~1. We'll include large etas.

Full gradient function:
```
def smooth_gradient(p, q, Nq=None, Ng=None):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    qmax=q.max()
    # temperature: want beta such that exp range maybe 10
    # If qmax-qmin small, use 0.1 qmax; if qmax zero, uniform
    span = float(q.max()-q.min())
    temp = max(span*0.25, qmax*0.05, 1e-30)
    w = np.exp((q - qmax)/temp)
    w /= w.sum()
    # w length L
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    # Need convolution p*w_rev full, take first L reversed first n
    Nconv = next_pow2(n + L - 1)
    full = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(p, Nconv) * np.fft.rfft(w_rev, Nconv), Nconv)
    r = full[:L][::-1][:n]
    w2 = w[0:L:2] # length n
    grad = r + p * w2
    return grad
```
 This uses logsumexp with temperature; if span=0, w uniform; grad uniform? p update no change. Good.
Need be careful with w length L, w_rev length L, Nconv pow2 >= n+L-1=3n-2. For n=16384, Nconv=1<<ceil(log2(49150))=65536? rfft ~ fast but extra.
Actually we can avoid rfft of p again? We have p rfft from q with Nq (pow2>=2n-1). But w length different; need Nconv. Fine.

Update in p-space:
```
def mirror_update(p, grad, eta):
    # grad scale O(1/n). If using smooth gradient, relative g=grad/p.mean.
    g = grad / (p.mean()+1e-300)
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300)) - eta * g
    newp = np.exp(logp - logp.max())
    s=newp.sum()
    if not finite or s==0 return None
    newp/=s
    return newp
```
For smooth gradient, eta can be [0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3,1.0,3.0]. Let's reason scale: uniform p, grad? If w uniform, r? w sum1, r_i = ∑ p_j w_{i+j} ≈1/n? grad~1/n, p.mean=1/n, g~1. So eta=0.1 changes 10%. Good. If p near optimum, small.

Additive update:
```
def additive_update(p, grad, eta):
   g = grad/(p.mean()+eps)
   newp = p - eta*g
   newp = np.clip(newp,0,None); normalize
```
But additive with eta large can zero; clip.

    except: pass

       if time > deadline-15 break
       p, sc = local_search(p, deadline_cand=min(deadline-10, time+?))
       if sc<best_score: update
    # maybe one final random block on best
    if time > deadline-5: break
    # exact validation of best: 
    try:
       exact = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in best_seq])
       print exact
    except: exact=best_score
    # If exact is nonfinite? return parent etc.
    # To be safe, if exact > best_score and top alternate has known exact? We didn't exact. Use best.
    return [float(x) for x in best_seq]

Implement `score_fft` and `convolve_fft` efficiently:
```
def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()

def convolve_fft(p, N=None):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    if N is None: N=next_pow2(L)
    P=np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    Q=np.fft.irfft(P*P, N)
    return Q[:L]

def score_fft(p, N=None):
    q=convolve_fft(p,N)
    return float(2*len(p)*np.max(q))
```
Because p sum=1, score objective. If p sum not exactly 1 due to normalization, still score_fft uses 2n max q, but evaluate_sequence would scale by sum^2. We normalize p sum1; after local update normalize. For candidates with amp>1 clipped, normalize. Good.

For exact evaluate, if we normalize sum1, exact score equals 2n max_q (within round). If we return p sum1, safe. Values <1000.

In local search, after mirror update p sum1. Use `float(np.sum(newp))`, if too small? no.

Pseudo code:

import numpy as np
import time, math

def next_pow2...
def normalize(p):
   p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64)
   p=np.clip(p,0,None)
   s=p.sum()
   if s<=1e-300: return None
   return p/s

def convolve_fft(p,N=None):...
def score_fft(p,N=None):...

def make_sine(n,r,amp,phase):
   i=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)
   vals=1.0 + amp*np.cos((2*math.pi*r/n)*i + phase)
   if vals.min()<0: vals=np.maximum(vals,0)
   s=vals.sum()
   if s<=1e-300: return None
   return vals/s

def scan_sine_family(deadline, initial_best_score, top_list):
   lengths = [2048,4096,8192,12288,16384]
   # Filter maybe n <= 20000
   for n in lengths:
      L=2*n-1; Nq=next_pow2(L)
      max_r=5 if n<=4096 else 4
      # Coarse phases: 129 from 0 to pi inclusive? phase of cos can be 0..2π; but cos period 2π. Use 0..2π? Since p=1+amp cos, phase period 2π. Use 0..2π 257? 0..π enough? cos over i covers full range; adding π flips sign, not same. 0..2π. Use 129? step ~0.049, okay. Use 257 for margin.
      phases = np.linspace(0, 2*math.pi, 257, endpoint=False)
      amps = [1.0, 0.9, 1.2] maybe, for n small all.
      for r in range(1,max_r+1):
         for amp in amps:
            for phase in phases:
               if time.monotonic()>deadline: break all
               p=make_sine(...)
               sc=score_fft(p,Nq)
               if sc<initial_best_score: update top
      # after coarse, maybe refine phases for top params? skip
   return top
```
Updating top: maintain list of top 8 entries: (score, p, n, r, amp, phase). If score lower than worst or list not full, insert sorted. Copy p because make returns new; if keep, no need copy? But if we later modify, yes `p.copy()`.
To avoid too many full scans, we can break per n if time. Use nested loops and check.

Refinement param:
```
def refine_params(params, score, p, deadline):
   n,r,amp,phase=params; Nq=...
   best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score
   # phase fine: step = 2π/(n*8?) maybe too fine; use delta = np.pi/(n)
   for _ in range(20):
      improved=False
      for delta in [ -8,-4,-2,-1,1,2,4,8 ]:
         pp=make_sine(n,r,amp,phase+delta*np.pi/n)
         if pp is not None:
            sc=score_fft(pp,Nq)
            if sc<best_score-1e-12:
               best_p=pp; best_score=sc; phase=phase+delta*...; improved=True
      for da in [ -0.02, -0.01, 0.01, 0.02]:
         pp=make_sine(n,r,amp+da,phase)
         ...
      if not improved: break
   # neighboring r
   for rr in [r-1,r,r+1] if within:
      # coarse phase around center
      center_phase = (math.pi - (2*math.pi*rr/n)*(n-1))*0.5
      for off in np.linspace(-0.2,0.2,21):
         pp=make_sine(n,rr,amp,center_phase+off)
         update
   return best_p,best_score
```

```
def smooth_gradient(p,q,Ngrad=None):
   ...

def local_search(p, deadline):
   n=len(p); Nq=next_pow2(2*n-1); Ngrad=next_pow2(3*n-2)
   best=p.copy(); best_score=score_fft(best,Nq)
   no_improve=0
   iteration=0
   while time.monotonic() < deadline:
      q=convolve_fft(best,Nq)
      q=np.maximum(q,0)
      grad=smooth_gradient(best,q,Ngrad)
      mean=p.mean() # best.mean
      improved=False
      # mirror
      for eta in (0.05,0.15,0.3,0.6,1.2,2.5):
         logp=np.log(np.maximum(best,1e-300))-eta*(grad/mean)
         cand=np.exp(logp-logp.max()); s=cand.sum(); cand/=s
         sc=score_fft(cand,Nq)
         if sc < best_score-1e-13:
             best=cand; best_score=sc; improved=True; break
      if not improved:
         for eta in (0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3):
             cand = best - eta*(grad/mean)
             cand=np.maximum(cand,0); s=cand.sum(); if s>0: cand/=s; sc=...
             if accept break
      if not improved:
         cand = block_perturb(best, rng? need rng global?)
```

Block perturb uses rng; if no_improve multiple, try 3 variants.
```
def block_perturb(p, rng, sigma=None):
   n=len(p); cand=p.copy(); sigma=0.08 if sigma is None else sigma
   for _ in range(4):
      width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//128), max(2,n//16)))
      start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
      cand[start:start+width] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma))
   cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); s=cand.sum(); if s==0 return None; cand/=s; return cand
```

Detailed code:

```
import numpy as np
import time
import math

def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()

def _normalize(p):
    if p is None: return None
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    p = p / s
    return p

def _convolve_fft(p, N=None):
    n = len(p)
    L = 2*n - 1
    if N is None:
        N = _next_pow2(L)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    Q = np.fft.irfft(P * P, N)
    return Q[:L]

def _score_fft(p, N=None):
    q = _convolve_fft(p, N)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    return float(2.0 * len(p) * float(np.max(q)))

def _make_sine(n, r, amp, phase):
    if n <=0: return None
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    val = 1.0 + amp * np.cos((2.0 * math.pi * r / float(n)) * i + phase)
    if amp > 1.0:
        val = np.maximum(val, 0.0)
    return _normalize(val)

def _make_top_list():
    return []

def _update_top(top, item, capacity=8):
    score, p, n, r, amp, phase = item
    top.append((score, p.copy(), n, r, amp, phase))
    top.sort(key=lambda x: x[0]) # lambda not allowed! Use sort with no lambda? We can use top.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))? Need import operator. Or avoid sorting by manual insert.
```
Rule: "Don't use any lambda functions." So no lambda. Use `import operator` and `operator.itemgetter(0)`? Or manual insert.
Manual insert:
```
def _update_top(top, item, capacity=8):
    if len(top) >= capacity:
        if item[0] >= top[-1][0]: return
        top.pop()
    ins = True
    for idx in range(len(top)):
        if item[0] < top[idx][0]:
            top.insert(idx, item)
            ins=False
            break
    if ins: top.append(item)
```
But if we pop last then insert maybe okay. Simpler: maintain list, for each item, if len<capacity or score < worst, insert sorted.

```
def _add_top(top, score, p, n, r, amp, phase, cap=8):
    if p is None: return
    if len(top) < cap:
        top.append((score, p.copy(), n, r, amp, phase))
    else:
        if score >= top[-1][0]:
            return
        # replace worst, then insert
        top.pop()
        top.append((score, p.copy(), n, r, amp, phase))
    # bubble to sorted
    i = len(top)-1
    while i > 0 and top[i][0] < top[i-1][0]:
        top[i], top[i-1] = top[i-1], top[i]
        i -= 1
```
If replacing worst, after append and bubble. But if top not sorted initially? It is.

In scan, for each score, if score < current worst. To avoid copying for scores not top, only copy if top. But make_sine returns p; if not top no memory. Need keep p for possible top. We can call _add_top with p. It copies if added. Good.

def _scan_sine_family(deadline):
    top=[]
    lengths = [2048, 4096, 8192, 12288, 16384]
    # maybe if deadline-now < 60 only small lengths
    for n in lengths:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       L=2*n-1; Nq=_next_pow2(L)
       max_r = 4
       amps = (1.0, 0.9, 1.15)
       # phase count maybe depends on n: 257
       nphase=257
       phases = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0*math.pi, nphase, endpoint=False)
       for r in range(1, max_r+1):
          for amp in amps:
             for phase in phases:
                if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
                p = _make_sine(n, r, amp, float(phase))
                if p is None: continue
                sc = _score_fft(p, Nq)
                if math.isfinite(sc):
                    _add_top(top, sc, p, n, r, amp, float(phase), 10)
             if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
          if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       # optional: after coarse for this n, refine top phases from this n? Could process later.
    return top
```

       center_phase = (math.pi - (2*math.pi*rr/n)*(n-1))*0.5
       for off in np.linspace(-0.25,0.25,21):
           pp=...

Local search:
```
def _smooth_gradient(p, q, Ngrad=None):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    qmax=float(np.max(q))
    if qmax <= 0:
        return np.zeros(n)
    span=float(np.max(q)-np.min(q))
    temp = max(0.05*span, 0.01*qmax, 1e-30)
    w = np.exp((q - qmax)/temp)
    sw = float(w.sum())
    if sw <=0 or not finite: w = np.full(L, 1.0/L)
    else: w /= sw
    if Ngrad is None: Ngrad = _next_pow2(n+L-1)
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    full = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(p, Ngrad) * np.fft.rfft(w_rev, Ngrad), Ngrad)
    r = full[:L][::-1][:n]
    w2 = w[0:L:2]
    return r + p * w2
```
Note `w[0:L:2]` length n, since L=2n-1. Good.

```
def _block_perturb(p, rng, sigma=0.1):
    n=len(p)
    cand=p.copy()
    for _ in range(4):
       width = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//16))) maybe if n//16 small
       start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width))
       cand[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
    cand=_normalize(cand)
    if cand is None: return None
    # optionally sym candidate
    return cand
```
In local loop, try multiple sigmas.

```
def _local_search(p, rng, deadline):
    n=len(p); Nq=_next_pow2(2*n-1); Ngrad=_next_pow2(3*n-2)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=_score_fft(best,Nq)
    no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       q=_convolve_fft(best,Nq)
       q=np.maximum(q,0.0)
       grad=_smooth_gradient(best,q,Ngrad)
       mean=float(best.mean()) + 1e-300
       improved=False
       # mirror updates
       for eta in (0.03,0.08,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0):
           g = grad / mean
           logp = np.log(np.maximum(best,1e-300)) - eta*g
           cand = np.exp(logp - float(np.max(logp)))
           s=float(cand.sum())
           if s > 0 and np.isfinite(s):
               cand/=s; sc=_score_fft(cand,Nq)
               if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                   best=cand; best_score=sc; improved=True; break
       if not improved:
           for eta in (0.005,0.01,0.03,0.1):
               cand = best - eta*(grad/mean)
               cand = np.maximum(cand,0); s=cand.sum(); if s>0:
                   cand/=s; sc=...
                   if accept break
       if not improved:
           # block
           for sigma in (0.03,0.08,0.16,0.32,0.64):
               cand=_block_perturb(best,rng,sigma)
               if cand is not None:
                  sc=_score_fft(cand,Nq)
                  if sc < best_score -1e-12: best=cand; best_score=sc; improved=True; break
              cand_s = 0.5*(cand+cand[::-1]); cand_s=_normalize(cand_s); score...
       if improved: no_improve=0
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve > 200:
              # bigger random restart from best
              cand=None
              for sigma in (0.5,1.0,2.0): ...
              if accept: no_improve=0
              else: break
    return best,best_score
```
Need rng. Use `rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)` in propose and pass.

One concern: gradient using smooth logsumexp may have temperature too low; if q near flat, temp small maybe weights uniform. If span zero, temp=0.01qmax, weights uniform. Good. If q has many equal peaks plateau (like 0.75), span small; weights uniform over all q, gradient may not target peaks specifically because weights based on q values that are nearly equal at max but lower elsewhere. Actually if max plateau 0.75 and lower elsewhere, span maybe 0.25; temp=0.05*span=0.0125 (in q units ~0.0125/n?), weights at max exp(0), at 0.5 exp(-20) negligible. Good. If q very flat below target but lower bound, span small; weights maybe moderate. Okay.

But local search with smooth gradient and mirror may slowly reduce; if starting near optimum, few steps. Could also try direct subgradient of max by using active peaks only; but full softmax okay.

```
def _block_perturb(p, rng, sigma):
   n=len(p)
   cand=p.copy()
   nmove=4
   for _ in range(nmove):
      maxw=max(2,n//16)
      width=int(rng.integers(1, maxw+1))
      start=int(rng.integers(0, n-width))
      cand[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
   cand = _normalize(cand)
   return cand
```

In local loop, after no_improve > 100, do random restarts with sigma larger; if no accept break to save time.

def _final_exact(seq):
   try:
      val = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in seq]))
      return val
   except Exception:
      return np.inf

    start = time.monotonic()

    # Let's exact top list if time and n<=8192 to choose by exact.

```python
"""
Nonnegative step-function search for the given convolution objective.

Main idea:
- The objective is scale invariant, so we always normalize sequences to sum 1.
- A very strong family of candidates is
      p_i = (1 + amp * cos(2*pi*r*i/n + phase)) / sum(...)
  with clipping to 0.  This gives smooth "raised-cosine" profiles whose
  linear convolution is close to the conjectured 1.5 lower envelope.
- We scan several lengths, frequencies, amplitudes and phases with FFT-based
  objective evaluation.
- Then we refine promising points by:
      * fine scans in the sinusoid parameters,
      * mirror-display style projected gradient descent using a smooth
        log-sum-exp surrogate for the max convolution coefficient,
      * randomized block perturbations for escape from tiny local minima.
- The best sequence found is returned as a Python list of nonnegative floats.
"""

import math
import time
import numpy as np


def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize_vector(p):
    if p is None:
        return None
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    return p / s


def _convolve_fft(p, N=None):
    """Full linear convolution p*p computed by FFT."""
    n = int(len(p))
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if N is None:
        N = _next_pow2(L)
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    Q = np.fft.irfft(P * P, N)
    return Q[:L]


def _score_fft(p, N=None):
    """Objective value when the sequence is normalized to sum 1."""
    q = _convolve_fft(p, N)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    return float(2.0 * len(p) * float(np.max(q)))


def _make_sine_sequence(n, r, amp, phase):
    """
    Build p_i = normalize(max(0, 1 + amp*cos(2*pi*r*i/n + phase))).
    The maximum is a harmless safety clip for amp > 1.
    """
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    omega = 2.0 * math.pi * float(r) / float(n)
    vals = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(omega * i + float(phase))
    vals = np.maximum(vals, 0.0)
    return _normalize_vector(vals)


def _add_top_entry(top, score, p, n, r, amp, phase, capacity=10):
    """Maintain a small sorted list of best candidates:
       (score, p_array, n, r, amp, phase).
    """
    if p is None or not math.isfinite(float(score)):
        return
    score = float(score)
    item = (score, p.copy(), int(n), int(r), float(amp), float(phase))

    if len(top) < capacity:
        top.append(item)
    else:
        if score >= top[-1][0]:
            return
        top.pop()
        top.append(item)

    # Bubble into sorted order.
    idx = len(top) - 1
    while idx > 0 and top[idx][0] < top[idx - 1][0]:
        top[idx], top[idx - 1] = top[idx - 1], top[idx]
        idx -= 1


def _scan_sine_family(deadline):
    """Coarse/global scan of the sinusoid family."""
    top = []

    # Lengths: large enough to approach the asymptotic bound, but not so
    # large that the final exact np.convolve evaluation becomes painful.
    lengths = [2048, 4096, 8192]

    # Keep the scan adaptive: start with a modest number of phases and then
    # locally interpolate later.  257 phases is already extremely cheap with FFT.
    nphases = 257
    phases = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi, nphases, endpoint=False)

    for n in lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

        L = 2 * n - 1
        Nq = _next_pow2(L)

        # r=1 is usually best, but a few neighboring frequencies can reduce
        # finite-length artifacts.
        max_r = 4
        amps = (1.0, 0.90, 1.15)

        for r in range(1, max_r + 1):
            for amp in amps:
                for phase in phases:
                    if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                        break
                    p = _make_sine_sequence(n, r, amp, float(phase))
                    if p is None:
                        continue
                    sc = _score_fft(p, Nq)
                    if math.isfinite(sc):
                        _add_top_entry(top, sc, p, n, r, amp, float(phase), capacity=12)
                if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                    break
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break

        if top:
            print(f"scan after n={n}: best_fft={top[0][0]:.7f} n={top[0][2]} r={top[0][3]}")

    return top


def _refine_sine_params(score, p, n, r, amp, phase, deadline):
    """Coordinate descent/local scan in (r, amp, phase) for a sinusoid candidate."""
    Nq = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    best_score = float(score)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_amp = float(amp)
    best_phase = float(phase)
    best_r = int(r)

    # Phase moves are tiny: O(1/n) is usually enough.
    base_step = math.pi / float(n)
    phase_deltas = (-10.0, -5.0, -2.0, -1.0, 1.0, 2.0, 5.0, 10.0)
    amp_deltas = (-0.05, -0.02, -0.01, 0.01, 0.02, 0.05)

    for _ in range(60):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        improved = False

        for d in phase_deltas:
            ph = best_phase + d * base_step
            pp = _make_sine_sequence(n, best_r, best_amp, ph)
            if pp is None:
                continue
            sc = _score_fft(pp, Nq)
            if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
                best_score = sc
                best_p = pp
                best_phase = ph
                improved = True

        for da in amp_deltas:
            aa = best_amp + da
            if aa < 0.25 or aa > 3.0:
                continue
            pp = _make_sine_sequence(n, best_r, aa, best_phase)
            if pp is None:
                continue
            sc = _score_fft(pp, Nq)
            if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
                best_score = sc
                best_p = pp
                best_amp = aa
                improved = True

        if not improved:
            break

    # Try neighboring integer frequencies, with a phase initially aligned so
    # that the coherent maximum is near the center of the convolution.
    for rr in (best_r - 1, best_r + 1):
        if rr < 1 or rr > 8:
            continue
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

        center_phase = 0.5 * (math.pi - (2.0 * math.pi * float(rr) / float(n)) * float(n - 1))
        offsets = np.linspace(-0.35, 0.35, 25)
        for off in offsets:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            ph = center_phase + float(off)
            pp = _make_sine_sequence(n, rr, best_amp, ph)
            if pp is None:
                continue
            sc = _score_fft(pp, Nq)
            if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
                best_score = sc
                best_p = pp
                best_amp = best_amp
                best_phase = ph
                best_r = rr

    return best_score, best_p


def _smooth_gradient(p, q, Ngrad=None):
    """
    Gradient of a smooth log-sum-exp surrogate for max(q), with respect to p.

    q is already p*p.  The derivative is
        g_i = sum_k w_k p_{k-i} + w_{2i} p_i
    where w is a normalized temperature-weighted distribution over q.
    """
    n = int(len(p))
    L = 2 * n - 1

    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    qmax = float(np.max(q))
    if qmax <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    span = float(np.max(q) - float(np.min(q)))
    temp = max(0.02 * span, 0.02 * qmax, 1e-30)

    w = np.exp((q - qmax) / temp)
    sw = float(w.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(sw) or sw <= 0.0:
        w = np.full(L, 1.0 / float(L), dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        w /= sw

    if Ngrad is None:
        Ngrad = _next_pow2(n + L - 1)

    # r_i = sum_{j valid} p_j * w_{i+j}.
    # This is (p * w_rev)[L-1-i] for i=0..n-1.
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    full = np.fft.irfft(
        np.fft.rfft(p, Ngrad) * np.fft.rfft(w_rev, Ngrad),
        Ngrad
    )
    r = full[:L][::-1][:n]

    # Extra diagonal term for k = 2i.
    w2 = w[0:L:2]
    return r + p * w2


def _mirror_update(p, grad, eta):
    g = grad / (float(p.mean()) + 1e-300)
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300)) - eta * g
    m = float(np.max(logp))
    cand = np.exp(logp - m)
    s = float(cand.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return cand / s


def _additive_update(p, grad, eta):
    g = grad / (float(p.mean()) + 1e-300)
    cand = p - eta * g
    return _normalize_vector(cand)


def _block_perturb(p, rng, sigma):
    n = int(len(p))
    cand = p.copy()
    moves = 4
    for _ in range(moves):
        maxw = max(2, n // 16)
        width = int(rng.integers(1, maxw + 1))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width))
        cand[start:start + width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
    return _normalize_vector(cand)


def _local_search(p, rng, deadline):
    """
    Local improvement for an already good candidate.

    Uses:
      - smooth gradient descent in mirror/softmax parameterization,
      - occasional additive steps,
      - randomized multiplicative block perturbations.
    """
    n = int(len(p))
    Nq = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    Ngrad = _next_pow2(3 * n - 2)

    best = p.copy()
    best_score = _score_fft(best, Nq)

    mirror_etap = (0.05, 0.15, 0.40, 1.00)
    additive_etap = (0.005, 0.015, 0.05)
    sigma_set = (0.04, 0.09, 0.18, 0.35, 0.70)

    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        q = _convolve_fft(best, Nq)
        q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
        grad = _smooth_gradient(best, q, Ngrad)

        improved = False

        # Mirror update.
        for eta in mirror_etap:
            cand = _mirror_update(best, grad, eta)
            if cand is None:
                continue
            sc = _score_fft(cand, Nq)
            if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
                best = cand
                best_score = sc
                improved = True
                break

        # Additive update as a backup.
        if not improved:
            for eta in additive_etap:
                cand = _additive_update(best, grad, eta)
                if cand is None:
                    continue
                sc = _score_fft(cand, Nq)
                if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
                    best = cand
                    best_score = sc
                    improved = True
                    break

        # Block perturbation to move away from smooth basins.
        if not improved:
            for sigma in sigma_set:
                cand = _block_perturb(best, rng, sigma)
                if cand is not None:
                    sc = _score_fft(cand, Nq)
                    if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
                        best = cand
                        best_score = sc
                        improved = True
                        break

                # Also try a symmetrized version; sometimes reduces one-sided peaks.
                sym = 0.5 * (cand + cand[::-1])
                sym = _normalize_vector(sym)
                if sym is not None:
                    sc = _score_fft(sym, Nq)
                    if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
                        best = sym
                        best_score = sc
                        improved = True
                        break

        if improved:
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 180:
                # One final aggressive kick, then stop to avoid wasting time.
                for sigma in (0.9, 1.6, 2.5):
                    cand = _block_perturb(best, rng, sigma)
                    if cand is None:
                        continue
                    sc = _score_fft(cand, Nq)
                    if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
                        best = cand
                        best_score = sc
                        improved = True
                        no_improve = 0
                        break

                if not improved:
                    break

    return best, best_score


def _sort_candidates(cands):
    """
    Sort list of (score, tiebreaker, sequence) without lambdas.
    """
    n = len(cands)
    if n <= 1:
        return cands

    for i in range(n - 1):
        best_i = i
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            if (
                cands[j][0] < cands[best_i][0]
                or (cands[j][0] == cands[best_i][0] and cands[j][1] < cands[best_i][1])
            ):
                best_i = j
        if best_i != i:
            cands[i], cands[best_i] = cands[best_i], cands[i]
    return cands


def _exact_score_safe(seq):
    try:
        vals = [float(x) for x in seq]
        return float(evaluate_sequence(vals))
    except Exception:
        return math.inf


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1500, **kwargs):
    """
    Entry point used by the harness.
    Returns a Python list of nonnegative floats.
    """
    budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 5.0)
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + budget

    rng = np.random.default_rng(1234567 if seed is None else int(seed))

    best_seq = None
    best_fft = math.inf

    # Keep the supplied parent sequence as a fallback.
    parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            ppar = _normalize_vector(np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64))
            if ppar is not None and ppar.size > 0:
                s = _score_fft(ppar)
                if math.isfinite(s):
                    best_seq = ppar
                    best_fft = s
                    print(f"parent_fft={s:.7f} n={len(ppar)}")
        except Exception:
            pass

    # If even the parent is invalid, start from a safe sinusoid.
    if best_seq is None:
        fb = _make_sine_sequence(2048, 1, 1.0, math.pi / 2.0)
        if fb is not None:
            best_seq = fb
            best_fft = _score_fft(fb)
            print(f"fallback_fft={best_fft:.7f} n={len(fb)}")

    # ---- Phase 1: scan sinusoid family -------------------------------
    # Reserve enough time for refinement/local search.
    scan_deadline = deadline - max(240.0, 0.30 * budget)
    top = []
    if time.monotonic() < scan_deadline:
        top = _scan_sine_family(scan_deadline)
        if top:
            print(f"scan_top[0]: score={top[0][0]:.7f}, n={top[0][2]}, r={top[0][3]}, amp={top[0][4]:.4f}, phase={top[0][5]:.6f}")
            if top[0][0] < best_fft - 1e-13:
                best_fft = top[0][0]
                best_seq = top[0][1]

    # ---- Phase 2: parameter refinement of promising points -----------
    refined_cands = []
    for idx, item in enumerate(top[:6]):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 150.0:
            break

        sc, p, n, r, amp, phase = item
        refine_deadline = min(deadline - 150.0, time.monotonic() + 25.0)
        rsc, rp = _refine_sine_params(sc, p, n, r, amp, phase, refine_deadline)

        if rp is not None and math.isfinite(rsc):
            refined_cands.append((rsc, idx, rp))
            if rsc < best_fft - 1e-13:
                best_fft = rsc
                best_seq = rp
                print(f"refined[{idx}] improved to {rsc:.7f}, n={len(rp)}")

    pad = []
    if refined_cands:
        for sc, p in [(s, p) for (s, _, p) in refined_cands]:
            pad.append((sc, p))
    else:
        for item in top[:3]:
            pad.append((item[0], item[1]))

    local_targets = []
    for i, (sc, p) in enumerate(pad):
        local_targets.append((sc, i, p))
    local_targets = _sort_candidates(local_targets)

    # ---- Phase 3: local search on the best few points ----------------
    time_assign = (350.0, 150.0, 80.0, 40.0)
    for i, (sc, _, p) in enumerate(local_targets[:4]):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 25.0:
            break

        allot = time_assign[i] if i < len(time_assign) else 30.0
        local_deadline = min(deadline - 15.0, time.monotonic() + allot)
        if local_deadline <= time.monotonic():
            continue

        lp, lsc = _local_search(p, rng, local_deadline)
        if lp is not None and math.isfinite(lsc) and lsc < best_fft - 1e-13:
            best_fft = lsc
            best_seq = lp
            print(f"local[{i}] improved to {lsc:.7f}, n={len(lp)}")

    # Final safety: if local search still has time, polish the current best.
    if best_seq is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 8.0:
        final_deadline = min(deadline - 2.0, time.monotonic() + 60.0)
        if final_deadline > time.monotonic():
            lp, lsc = _local_search(best_seq, rng, final_deadline)
            if lp is not None and math.isfinite(lsc) and lsc < best_fft - 1e-13:
                best_fft = lsc
                best_seq = lp
                print(f"final polish improved to {lsc:.7f}")

    # Fallback if everything went wrong.
    if best_seq is None or len(best_seq) == 0:
        fb = _make_sine_sequence(2048, 1, 1.0, math.pi / 2.0)
        if fb is not None:
            best_seq = fb
            best_fft = _score_fft(fb)

    # Exact validation.  The returned sequence is the one selected by the
    # FFT objective; exact evaluation should agree up to rounding.
    exact_val = _exact_score_safe(best_seq)
    print(f"final_fft={best_fft:.7f}, final_exact={exact_val:.7f}, final_n={len(best_seq)}")

    return [float(x) for x in best_seq]
```
